      54 +def _window(details: str) -> tuple[timedelta, timedelta]:      
      55 +    earliest, latest = next((w for marker, w in TEMPLATE_WINDOW
         +S if marker in details), DEFAULT_WINDOW)                       
      56 +    return timedelta(hours=earliest), timedelta(hours=latest)

      59 +def identified_resources(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> set[str]:
      60 +    """Resources that some observation ties to a known person b
         +y identifier, at any time."""                                  
      61 +    placeholders = ", ".join("?" for _ in LINK_KINDS)          
      62 +    rows = conn.execute(                                       
      63 +        f"""SELECT DISTINCT o.resource_id FROM obs_identifiers 
         +oi                                                             
      64 +            JOIN observations o USING (obs_id)                 
      65 +            JOIN identifiers i ON i.kind = oi.kind AND i.value 
         += oi.value                                                     
      66 +            WHERE oi.kind IN ({placeholders}) AND o.resource_id
         + IS NOT NULL""",                                               
      67 +        LINK_KINDS,                                            
      68 +    )                                                          
      69 +    return {row["resource_id"] for row in rows}

      72 +def _fits(row: sqlite3.Row, request: sqlite3.Row) -> bool:     
      73 +    earliest, latest = _window(row["details"])                 
      74 +    reported = _time(request["reported_at"])                   
      75 +    if not reported + earliest <= _time(row["event_time"]) <= r
         +eported + latest:                                              
      76 +        return False                                           
      77 +    if PROFILE_TEMPLATE in row["details"]:                     
      78 +        return PROFILE_RESULTS.get(request["reported_status"]) 
         +== row["reported_result"]                                      
      79 +    return True

● The LangGraph pipeline now runs end to end and has a command for each file. On
  the 32 samples it gets every status right (32/32), and every step it produces
  matches the answer key. It finds 93% of the expected steps. The README is at
  code/README.md. Nothing is committed.

  Commands (run from the repo root):
  - uv run --project code python code/main.py run audits the 518 requests and
    writes output.csv.
  - uv run --project code python code/main.py sample audits the 32 samples,
    writes output_sample.csv, scores it, and writes the per-case differences to
    code/.cache/sample_diff.txt.
  - uv run --project code python code/main.py score re-scores an existing
    output_sample.csv.
  - Add --no-llm to run or sample to use only cached model results. Once the
    caches exist, the full run takes about 3 seconds and makes no model calls,
    and two reruns produced byte-identical output.csv files.

  The five steps:
  1. Classify: requests go to the model 20 at a time and each answer is cached.
     It flagged exactly 10 of the 550 requests as not asking for deletion
     (corrections, data copies, marketing opt-outs). That matches the 10
     "classified as non-deletion" rows in the activity log.
  2. State at audit_at, per copy: the primary and each replica are judged
     separately, so a leftover replica row is still a finding. Two rules were
     needed to match the answer key:
     - A direct sighting of a record outranks the record merely being missing
       from a snapshot listing, unless the sighting is from before the request.
     - Evidence is cited only if the file was captured by audit_at. A record can
       still be linked to the case from a log line written before audit_at,
       even if the log was exported later.
  3. Rules: each record's category comes from the evidence when stated,
     otherwise from its id prefix and fields. It then gets a verdict: compliant,
     violation, or unknown.
  4. Steps: built in the required order and checked against the output contract.
  5. Scoring:

  ┌──────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────┐
  │            Metric            │         Value         │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ Status accuracy              │ 1.000                 │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ Step precision / recall / F1 │ 1.000 / 0.933 / 0.965 │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ Exact full-sequence match    │ 21/32                 │
  └──────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────┘

  Linking deleted accounts: a deleted account or anonymized analytics record no
  longer carries any identifier. Its only trace is an anonymous row in the
  activity log, timed around the case's reported_at. A new module matches those
  rows to cases across all 550 requests at once, but only when exactly one case
  fits. That raised step recall on the samples from 0.86 to 0.93 without adding
  any wrong steps.

  What's still missing:
  - Undiscoverable: three expected records (account_004, audit_record_6645,
    audit_record_5569) appear nowhere in dataset/ except the answer key, so no
    method can find them.
  - Ambiguous: the other misses are activity rows that several cases sharing the
    same reported_at could own. I leave those unassigned rather than guess.
    Because of this, 118 of the 508 eval deletion cases have no account step.
  - case_61: it comes out COMPLETE with no steps, because its only linked record
    has activity claims but no evidence of its state.

  Decision for you: within one case, these rows are spaced by fixed intervals
  down to the second. Matching on that would likely resolve most of the
  ambiguous ones, but it exploits a quirk of how the dataset was generated
  rather than real audit reasoning. I left it out. Say if you want it.

  Notes:
  - README location: code.zip also includes the root README.md, which is still
    the organizers' starter text. You may want to replace it with this one or
    point it here.
  - Packaging: the README's zip command excludes code/.cache/, which holds a
    database rebuilt from the dataset. That means graders need an API key, or
    you ship the two cache files on purpose.
  - Still to write: code/evaluation/evaluation_report.md (including at least
    three custom test cases) and code/evaluation/usage_report.md. Model usage so
    far, logged per call, is about USD 0.58 in total.

● Both files now live in code/ (code/output.csv and code/output_sample.csv), and
  I deleted the old copies at the repo root. Rows were already sorted by case
  number (case_33 … case_550, case_01 … case_32), not alphabetically. The CSV
  writer enforces that, so it stays that way. Sample scores are unchanged.

  Here are the open problems, most impactful first, each with what I'd do about
  it.

  1. AGENTS.md wants output.csv at the repo root

  Section 6.7 of AGENTS.md says the finished output.csv must be written at the
  repository root. The challenge README says the same.
  Suggestion: keep code/output.csv as the main file and also write a copy to the
  root during run. Since you upload output.csv separately on HackerRank,
  code/output.csv alone would also work. Your call: add the root copy or not?

  2. Deleted accounts and anonymized analytics records that can't be linked
  (biggest gap)

  - The problem: a deleted account or an anonymized analytics record no longer
    carries any identifier. The only trace is an anonymous deletion-worker row
    in the activity log.
  - How bad it is:
    - 408 such records exist; the correlation step matches 189 of them to a case
      (168 accounts, 21 analytics records).
    - 118 of the 508 deletion cases have no account step at all.
    - 93 of those 118 share their reported_at with two other cases, so timing
      alone can't separate them.
    - Almost all anonymized analytics records (105 of 126) stay unmatched.
  - What it costs: in COMPLETE cases these records would have been
    evidence-backed checks, so each miss loses one step.
  - Suggestion: rows from the same deletion run line up to the second. In all 5
    sample cases I checked, the analytics row came exactly N minutes and 41
    seconds after the account row. Matching on that would likely clear most of
    the ambiguity, and the 32 samples would show whether it adds wrong links.
    The catch is that this exploits how the dataset was generated rather than
    real audit reasoning, which you'd want to say openly in the AI interview.
  - Your call: try it, or keep the conservative behaviour?

  3. Records that don't exist anywhere in participant data

  account_004, audit_record_6645 and audit_record_5569 appear only in the sample
  answer key. About 10 early eval cases (case_33–63, e.g. case_33, 39, 52) look
  built the same way, with no account row anywhere.
  Suggestion: nothing to fix. List them as a known dataset limitation in the
  evaluation report.

  4. case_61 answers COMPLETE with an empty step list

  Its only record, object_19, has a catalog entry before audit_at saying the
  attachment belongs only to this user, and nothing showing it was deleted. So
  we can't prove anything, yet the answer is COMPLETE with [].
  Suggestion: treat catalog-link rows as proof the record existed at that time.
  case_61 would then become INCOMPLETE, reporting object_19 as a remaining
  record and citing activity_4007. Only 12 such rows exist, so the effect is
  small and easy to check.

  5. One support record is probably in the wrong category

  support_1034 (case_53) shows no attachment or message cues, so it falls back
  to "attachment → delete". But the deletion worker itself tried to redact it (a
  REDACT_RECORD row), which suggests it's a support message that should be
  redacted.
  Suggestion: when no field identifies the category, use the type of operation
  the deletion worker attempted as the hint. It's a small, principled change.

  6. The "listing vs direct sighting" rule rests on two sample cases

  - The rule: when a record is missing from a snapshot listing but a direct
    sighting shows it, the sighting wins unless it's from before the request.
  - Why it's fragile: I took that from case_24 and case_31 only. 200 compliant
    verdicts in the eval run rely on a listing to prove a record is gone. If
    some listings are partial, those could be false "absent" results.
  - Suggestion: this is a good place for the custom test cases the evaluation
    report needs. Build two or three small cases where the rule is stressed (an
    old direct sighting followed by a newer listing, and the reverse) and make
    sure each verdict is the one we intend.

  7. The request classifier has no labelled check

  None of the 32 samples is a non-deletion request, so the sample score can't
  measure the classifier. A good sign: it flagged exactly 10 of 550 requests,
  which matches the 10 "classified as non-deletion" rows in the activity log.
  Suggestion: add that agreement as a run-time warning (without letting it
  decide anything), and add custom test cases for tricky wording, such as a
  marketing opt-out that uses the word "delete".

  8. The required evaluation reports aren't written

  code/evaluation/evaluation_report.md and usage_report.md are both required.
  The usage report has to describe the final full run, but with the caches in
  place a run makes no model calls.
  Suggestion: once the fixes above are settled, clear the caches and do one cold
  run. Then generate both reports from the scorer and the per-call token log,
  so the numbers are real.

  The order I'd suggest: decide on 1, fix 4 and 5 (small and principled), decide
  on 2, then build the test cases (6 and 7) and the reports (8).

      100 +    """Candidate sets and forced assignments for anonymous wor
          +ker resources."""                                             
      101 +                                                              
      102 +    def __init__(self, rows_by_resource, candidates, case_reso
          +urces, worker_rows):                                          
      103 +        self.rows = rows_by_resource                 # anonymo
          +us resource -> worker rows                                    
      104 +        self.candidates = candidates                 # anonymo
          +us resource -> candidate cases                                
      105 +        self.case_resources = case_resources         # case ->
          + resources known to be its own                                
      106 +        self.worker_rows = worker_rows               # any res
          +ource -> worker rows                                          
      107 +        self.assigned: dict[str, str] = {}                    
      108 +        self.offsets: dict[str, set[int]] = {}                
      109 +                                                              
      110 +    def assign(self, resource_id: str, case_id: str) -> None: 
      111 +        self.assigned[resource_id] = case_id                  
      112 +        self.case_resources[case_id].add(resource_id)         
      113 +        kind = resource_type(resource_id)                     
      114 +        for other, options in self.candidates.items():        
      115 +            if other not in self.assigned and resource_type(ot
          +her) == kind:                                                 
      116 +                options.discard(case_id)                      
      117 +                                                              
      118 +    def propagate(self) -> bool:                              
      119 +        changed = False                                       
      120 +        progress = True                                       
      121 +        while progress:                                       
      122 +            progress = False                                  
      123 +            for resource_id, options in sorted(self.candidates
          +.items()):                                                    
      124 +                if resource_id not in self.assigned and len(op
          +tions) == 1:                                                  
      125 +                    self.assign(resource_id, next(iter(options
          +)))                                                           
      126 +                    progress = changed = True                 
      127 +        return changed                                        
      128 +                                                              
      129 +    def anchor(self, case_id: str) -> datetime | None:        
      130 +        """Time of the case's primary-profile row, if exactly 
          +one is known."""                                              
      131 +        times = {                                             
      132 +            _time(row["event_time"])                          
      133 +            for resource_id in self.case_resources[case_id] if
          + resource_type(resource_id) == "account"                      
      134 +            for row in self.worker_rows.get(resource_id, ()) i
          +f PROFILE_TEMPLATE in row["details"]                          
      135 +        }                                                     
      136 +        return next(iter(times)) if len(times) == 1 else None 
      137 +                                                              
      138 +    def calibrate(self) -> None:                              
      139 +        """Learn the cadence offsets from cases whose rows are
          + attributed with certainty."""                                
      140 +        observed: dict[str, Counter] = defaultdict(Counter)   
      141 +        for case_id, resources in self.case_resources.items():
      142 +            anchor = self.anchor(case_id)                     
      143 +            if anchor is None:                                
      144 +                continue                                      
      145 +            for resource_id in resources:                     
      146 +                if resource_type(resource_id) == "account":   
      147 +                    continue                                  
      148 +                for row in self.worker_rows.get(resource_id, (
          +)):                                                           
      149 +                    observed[_row_class(row)][_offset(row, anc
          +hor)] += 1                                                    
      150 +        self.offsets = {                                      
      151 +            row_class: {offset for offset, n in counts.items()
          + if n >= OFFSET_MIN_SHARE * sum(counts.values())}             
      152 +            for row_class, counts in observed.items() if sum(c
          +ounts.values()) >= CALIBRATION_MIN_ROWS                       
      153 +        }                                                     
      154 +                                                              
      155 +    def _on_cadence(self, rows, anchor: datetime) -> bool:    
      156 +        return all(                                           
      157 +            _offset(row, anchor) in self.offsets[_row_class(ro
          +w)]                                                           
      158 +            for row in rows if _row_class(row) in self.offsets
          + and PROFILE_TEMPLATE not in row["details"]                   
      159 +        )                                                     
      160 +                                                              
      161 +    def prune(self) -> bool:                                  
      162 +        """Drop candidate cases whose known workflow cadence c
          +ontradicts the resource's rows."""                            
      163 +        changed = False                                       
      164 +        for resource_id, options in self.candidates.items():  
      165 +            if resource_id in self.assigned:                  
      166 +                continue                                      
      167 +            rows = self.rows[resource_id]                     
      168 +            for case_id in sorted(options):                   
      169 +                if resource_type(resource_id) == "account":   
      170 +                    profile = [r for r in rows if PROFILE_TEMP
          +LATE in r["details"]]                                         
      171 +                    if len(profile) != 1:                     
      172 +                        continue                              
      173 +                    anchor = _time(profile[0]["event_time"])  
      174 +                    others = [row for other in self.case_resou
          +rces[case_id] if resource_type(other) != "account"            
      175 +                              for row in self.worker_rows.get(
          +other, ())]                                                   
      176 +                    fits = self._on_cadence(others, anchor)   
      177 +                else:                                         
      178 +                    anchor = self.anchor(case_id)             
      179 +                    fits = anchor is None or self._on_cadence(
          +rows, anchor)                                                 
      180 +                if not fits:                                  
      181 +                    options.discard(case_id)                  
      182 +                    changed = True                            
      183 +        return changed                                        
      184 +                                                              
      185 +

● I'll narrow the catalog-link rule so it only counts as a sighting when a
  resource has no other state evidence, since treating it broadly caused 6 wrong
  citations and 2 lost findings by displacing real evidence like screenshots.

       62 +def cross_check(conn: sqlite3.Connection, requests: list[sqlit
          +e3.Row],                                                      
       63 +                intents: dict[str, RequestClassification]) -> 
          +list[str]:                                                    
       64 +    """Compare the model's decisions with the privacy-intake r
          +ows of the activity log.                                      
       65 +                                                              
       66 +    Intake logs "classified request for account_X as non-delet
          +ion" while a request                                          
       67 +    is open. Those rows are claims and never decide a case, bu
          +t a disagreement is                                           
       68 +    worth a human look. Returns one warning line per disagreem
          +ent.                                                          
       69 +    """                                                       
       70 +    owners = {}                                               
       71 +    for row in conn.execute(                                  
       72 +        """SELECT DISTINCT a.activity_id, a.event_time, i.user
          +_id FROM activity a                                           
       73 +           JOIN observations o ON o.resource_id = a.resource_i
          +d                                                             
       74 +           JOIN obs_identifiers oi ON oi.obs_id = o.obs_id    
       75 +           JOIN identifiers i ON i.kind = oi.kind AND i.value 
          += oi.value                                                    
       76 +           WHERE a.event_type = 'CLASSIFY_REQUEST' AND i.kind 
          +IN ('user_id', 'email', 'email_hash', 'phone')"""             
       77 +    ):                                                        
       78 +        owners.setdefault((row["activity_id"], row["event_time
          +"]), set()).add(row["user_id"])                               
       79 +                                                              
       80 +    users = {                                                 
       81 +        row["case_id"]: row["user_id"]                        
       82 +        for row in conn.execute(                              
       83 +            "SELECT DISTINCT r.case_id, i.user_id FROM request
          +s r "                                                         
       84 +            "JOIN identifiers i ON i.kind = 'email' AND i.valu
          +e = lower(r.email)"                                           
       85 +        )                                                     
       86 +    }                                                         
       87 +    flagged_non_deletion = set()                              
       88 +    for (activity_id, event_time), user_ids in owners.items():
       89 +        for request in requests:                              
       90 +            if users.get(request["case_id"]) in user_ids and r
          +equest["requested_at"] <= event_time <= request["reported_at"]
          +:                                                             
       91 +                flagged_non_deletion.add(request["case_id"])  
       92 +                                                              
       93 +    warnings = []                                             
       94 +    for request in requests:                                  
       95 +        model_says = intents[request["request_text"]].authoriz
          +es_deletion                                                   
       96 +        intake_says = request["case_id"] not in flagged_non_de
          +letion                                                        
       97 +        if model_says != intake_says:                         
       98 +            warnings.append(                                  
       99 +                f"{request['case_id']}: model says authorizes_
          +deletion={model_says}, privacy intake log says {intake_says}" 
      100 +            )                                                 
      101 +    return warnings

      205  
      206  ### 4.6 Policy (`audit/policy.py`)
      207  
      208 -The data category comes from the evidence's `record_type` when
          - a source states one. Otherwise it is inferred from the resour
          -ce-id prefix and the record's fields, for example `file_name`/
          -`uploaded_by` → support attachment and `message_text` → suppor
          -t message.                                                    
      208 +The data category comes from the evidence's `record_type` when
          + a source states one. Otherwise it is inferred from the resour
          +ce-id prefix and the record's own fields:                     
      209  
      210 +- `file_name`/`uploaded_by` → support attachment;             
      211 +- `message_text`, or a document describing a ticket or convers
          +ation → support message.                                      
      212 +                                                              
      213 +If nothing in the record decides it, the agent falls back on t
          +he operation the deletion workflow attempted on the record (re
          +dact vs delete), which shows how the platform itself classifie
          +d it.                                                         
      214 +                                                              
      215 +A catalog entry (`LINK_RESOURCE_IDENTITY`) proves that a recor
          +d existed, and nothing more. It is used as evidence only when 
          +no other source shows the record's state.                     
      216 +                                                              
      217  | Category | Rule | Compliant when |
      218  | --- | --- | --- |
      219  | USER_PROFILE, SEARCH_INDEX_ENTRY, CACHE_ENTRY, USER_EXPORT, 
           MARKETING_PROFILE, SUPPORT_ATTACHMENT, SECONDARY_IDENTIFIER | 
           DELETE | every copy is absent |

  Problem 2, linking deleted accounts. Rows from one deletion run keep a fixed
  rhythm down to the second. The offsets aren't hard-coded: each run learns them
  from rows whose case is already certain (99% of 639 such rows fall on four
  offsets), and uses them only to rule out wrong candidates. The 5 remaining
  sample misses are three records that appear nowhere in the data except the
  answer key, and two rows that more than one case could still own, which I
  leave unassigned rather than guess.

  Problems 4 and 5, wrong answers fixed. Each fix changed exactly one eval
  answer and no sample answer:
  - case_61 is now INCOMPLETE. A catalog entry is used as proof a record exists
    only when nothing else shows its state. My first version also let catalog
    rows override real evidence and changed 8 other cases, so I narrowed it
    before keeping it.
  - case_53's support ticket is now redacted instead of deleted. The screenshot
    shows a conversation with agent and order context, which is exactly the
    redact rule.

  Problem 7, classifier check. The model's 10 non-deletion requests match the 10
  "classified as non-deletion" rows in the activity log, with zero
  disagreements. Every run prints this check; it only warns and never changes an
  answer.

  Problems 6 and 8, test cases and reports.
  - evaluate command: it runs the sample scoring plus 8 hand-built audit cases
    and 6 tricky classifier wordings; all pass. When I switched off the listing
    rule and then the repeat-request rule, the case aimed at each one failed, so
    the tests do catch regressions.
  - code/evaluation/usage_report.md: written automatically by run. It comes from
    a cold run with all caches cleared: 35 model calls, 153,158 tokens, about
    $0.54. The output was byte-identical to the cached run.
  - code/evaluation/evaluation_report.md: has every metric the challenge asks
    for, the test cases, a table of each improvement with its scores, and the
    known gaps.

  Before you submit:
  - Re-running run resets the usage report. Every run rewrites the report from
    the calls it made, so a cached re-run reports zero calls. To refresh it,
    delete code/.cache/ and run once more; I kept a backup of the old caches in
    the scratchpad.
  - Disclose the timing heuristic. The rhythm in Problem 2 most likely comes
    from how the dataset was generated, not from real audit reasoning. I'd
    mention it openly in the AI interview; the report already describes it.
  - Check the cost figure. It uses the per-token prices in code/audit/llm.py,
    which an earlier session set and I didn't re-check.
